C.2 Architecture and Optimization Details




For all experiments, we consider the architecture inspired by GPT-2 as used in Raventós et al. (2023).
For linear regression experiments, we use a context length of 64 points, 6 layers, embedding dimension of 32, 8 attention heads and an output dimension of 1.
For the other experiments, we use a context length of 32 points, 8 layers, embedding dimension of 128, 2 attention heads and an output dimension of 8.




All models were trained for 5\times 10^{5}5×1055\times 10^{5} iterations.
Experiments are run with AdamW optimizer with a weight decay of 0.1 with a cosine learning rate schedule and 50,000 warmup steps.
All experiments were run on NVIDIA H100 GPUs.
We performed a hyperparameter sweep over learning rate where we considered two learning rates and chose the best model.
Experiments are repeated 3 different times with different seeds.
LLMs were used to assist in code writing.










Appendix D Task Selection




In this section, we study how tasks are selected at test time in ICL.
This section is structured as follows.
First we consider an abstract setting for Sections D.1 and D.2 where in Section D.1 we state a few preliminary lemmas that will be useful in the analysis, and in Section D.2 we prove a template task selection bound under minimal assumptions.
Then, in Section D.3, we reintroduce the ICL setting along with the detailed assumptions before proving the main task selection bound in Section D.4, which is where the main contribution of this section lies.





D.1 Preliminary Lemmas



Definition 1 (Kullback-Leibler divergence).


For \probℙ\prob and \probaltℚ\probalt two probability measures on a measurable space {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}𝒳{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}, the Kullback-Leibler (KL) divergence from \probℙ\prob to \probaltℚ\probalt is defined as






{\mathrm{KL}}\!(\prob\,\|\,\probalt)=\begin{cases}\int_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}}\log\left(\frac{d\prob}{d\probalt}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x})\right)d\prob({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x})&\text{if }\prob\ll\probalt\\
+\infty&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}KL(ℙ∥ℚ)={∫𝒳log⁡(d​ℙd​ℚ​(x))​d​ℙ⁡(x)if ​ℙ≪ℚ+∞otherwise.{\mathrm{KL}}\!(\prob\,\|\,\probalt)=\begin{cases}\int_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}}\log\left(\frac{d\prob}{d\probalt}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x})\right)d\prob({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x})&\text{if }\prob\ll\probalt\\
+\infty&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

(D.1)






We now state the Donsker-Varadhan lemma, also known as the Gibbs variational principle.



Lemma D.1 (Donsker-Varadhan lemma, Gibbs variational principle).


Consider \probℙ\prob probability measure on a measurable {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}𝒳{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}} and {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}g:𝒳→ℝ{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}} a measurable function such that \ex_{\prob}[\exp({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g})]<\infty𝔼ℙ⁡[exp⁡(g)]<∞\ex_{\prob}[\exp({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g})]<\infty. Then, we have






\log\ex_{\prob}[e^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x})}]=\sup_{\probalt}\left\{\ex_{\probalt}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x})]-{\mathrm{KL}}\!(\probalt\,\|\,\prob)\right\},log𝔼ℙ[eg⁡(x)]=supℚ{𝔼ℚ[g(x)]−KL(ℚ∥ℙ)},\log\ex_{\prob}[e^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x})}]=\sup_{\probalt}\left\{\ex_{\probalt}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x})]-{\mathrm{KL}}\!(\probalt\,\|\,\prob)\right\},

(D.2)



with equality attained in particular for \frac{d\probalt}{d\prob}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x})\propto e^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x})}d​ℚd​ℙ​(x)∝eg⁡(x)\frac{d\probalt}{d\prob}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x})\propto e^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x})}.





See for instance Hellström et al. (2025); Rodríguez-Gálvez et al. (2024) for original references and proofs.




Let us state a technical consequence of this lemma that essentially corresponds to Zhang (2003, Lem. 3.1).



Lemma D.2.


Consider {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}X{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X} a random variable on {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}𝒳{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}} distributed according to \prob_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}ℙX\prob_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}
and {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}θ{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta} a random variable on {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta}Θ{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta} with prior distribution {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}(d{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})π⁡(d​θ){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}(d{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}) and with posterior distribution such that,
conditionally on {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}X{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X},






\widehat{\prob}(d{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X})=\frac{d\prob({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}{d\prob({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X})}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}(d{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})\,.ℙ^(dθ|X)=dℙ(X|θ)d​ℙ⁡(X)π(dθ).\widehat{\prob}(d{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X})=\frac{d\prob({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}{d\prob({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X})}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}(d{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})\,.

(D.3)



Consider {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}\times{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}}L:𝒳×Θ→ℝ{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}\times{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}} a measurable function.
Then,






\ex_{#1}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})-\log\ex_{#1}\left[\exp({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}))\right]\right]\leq\ex_{#1}\left[{\mathrm{KL}}\!(\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X})\,\|\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi})\right]\,.𝔼#​1[L(X,θ)−log𝔼#​1[exp(L(X,θ))]]≤𝔼#​1[KL(ℙ#​1(⋅|X)∥π)].\ex_{#1}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})-\log\ex_{#1}\left[\exp({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}))\right]\right]\leq\ex_{#1}\left[{\mathrm{KL}}\!(\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X})\,\|\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi})\right]\,.

(D.4)





Proof.


We apply Lemma D.1 with {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})-\log\ex_{#1}\left[\exp({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}))\right]g⁡(θ)=L⁡(X,θ)−log⁡𝔼#​1​[exp⁡(L⁡(X,θ))]{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})-\log\ex_{#1}\left[\exp({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}))\right] conditionally on {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}X{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X} to obtain







\displaystyle\ex_{#1}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})-\log\ex_{#1}\left[\exp({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}))\right]-{\mathrm{KL}}\!(\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X})\,\|\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi})\right]𝔼#​1[L(X,θ)−log𝔼#​1[exp(L(X,θ))]−KL(ℙ#​1(⋅|X)∥π)]\displaystyle\ex_{#1}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})-\log\ex_{#1}\left[\exp({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}))\right]-{\mathrm{KL}}\!(\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X})\,\|\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi})\right]

(D.5)





\displaystyle\leq\log\ex_{#1}\left[\exp\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})-\log\ex_{#1}\left[\exp({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}))\right]\right)\right]\,.≤log⁡𝔼#​1​[exp⁡(L⁡(X,θ)−log⁡𝔼#​1​[exp⁡(L⁡(X,θ))])].\displaystyle\leq\log\ex_{#1}\left[\exp\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})-\log\ex_{#1}\left[\exp({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}))\right]\right)\right]\,.

(D.6)



We then have







\displaystyle\ex_{#1}\left[\exp\ex_{#1}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})-\log\ex_{#1}\left[\exp({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}))\right]-{\mathrm{KL}}\!(\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X})\,\|\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi})\right]\right]𝔼#​1[exp𝔼#​1[L(X,θ)−log𝔼#​1[exp(L(X,θ))]−KL(ℙ#​1(⋅|X)∥π)]]\displaystyle\ex_{#1}\left[\exp\ex_{#1}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})-\log\ex_{#1}\left[\exp({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}))\right]-{\mathrm{KL}}\!(\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X})\,\|\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi})\right]\right]

(D.7)





\displaystyle\leq\ex_{#1}\left[\exp\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})-\log\ex_{#1}\left[\exp({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}))\right]\right)\right]=1\,,≤𝔼#​1⁡[exp⁡(L⁡(X,θ)−log⁡𝔼#​1​[exp⁡(L⁡(X,θ))])]=1,\displaystyle\leq\ex_{#1}\left[\exp\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})-\log\ex_{#1}\left[\exp({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}))\right]\right)\right]=1\,,

(D.8)



and the result follows by Jensen’s inequality with the convex function \expexp\exp.
∎








D.2 Template Task Selection Bound




Let us start with a template task selection bound under minimal assumptions. This proof is adapted from Zhang (2003, Thm. 4.1) to the case of non-i.i.d. data and when the true task is not necessarily in the support of the prior.



Proposition D.1 (Template task selection bound).


Consider {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}X{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X} a random variable on {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}}𝒳{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{X}} distributed according to \prob_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}ℙX\prob_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}
and {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}θ{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta} a random variable on {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta}Θ{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta} with prior distribution {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}(d{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})π⁡(d​θ){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}(d{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}) such that,
conditionally on {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}X{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}, {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}θ{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta} is distributed according to






\widehat{\prob}(d{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X})=\frac{d\prob({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}{d\prob({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X})}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}(d{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})\,.ℙ^(dθ|X)=dℙ(X|θ)d​ℙ⁡(X)π(dθ).\widehat{\prob}(d{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X})=\frac{d\prob({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}{d\prob({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X})}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}(d{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})\,.

(D.9)



Then, we have, for any {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta}θ0∈Θ{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta},
for any {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}\in(0,1)ρ∈(0,1){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}\in(0,1), {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha}>1α>1{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha}>1,







\displaystyle\ex_{#1}\left[-\log\ex_{#1}\left[\left(\frac{d\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}{d\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot)}\right)^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}}\right]\right]𝔼#​1⁡[−log⁡𝔼#​1⁡[(dℙ#​1(⋅|θ)d​ℙ#​1​(⋅))ρ]]\displaystyle\ex_{#1}\left[-\log\ex_{#1}\left[\left(\frac{d\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}{d\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot)}\right)^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}}\right]\right]

(D.10)





\displaystyle\leq-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha}{\log\ex_{#1}\left[\exp\left(-\ex_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}{\log\frac{d\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}{d\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}}\right)\right]}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha}{\mathrm{KL}}\!\left(\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot)\,\|\,\prob_{\!#1}\left(\cdot\nonscript\>\middle|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}}\right)\right)≤−αlog𝔼#​1[exp(−𝔼Xlogdℙ#​1(⋅|θ0)dℙ#​1(⋅|θ))]+αKL(ℙ#​1(⋅)∥ℙ#​1(⋅|θ0))\displaystyle\leq-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha}{\log\ex_{#1}\left[\exp\left(-\ex_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}{\log\frac{d\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}{d\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}}\right)\right]}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha}{\mathrm{KL}}\!\left(\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot)\,\|\,\prob_{\!#1}\left(\cdot\nonscript\>\middle|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}}\right)\right)

(D.11)





\displaystyle\quad+({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha}-1)\ex_{#1}\left[\log\ex_{#1}\left[\exp\left(-\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha}-1}\log\frac{d\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}{d\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}\right)\right]\right]+(α−1)​𝔼#​1​[log⁡𝔼#​1​[exp⁡(−α−ρα−1​log⁡dℙ#​1(⋅|θ0)dℙ#​1(⋅|θ))]]\displaystyle\quad+({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha}-1)\ex_{#1}\left[\log\ex_{#1}\left[\exp\left(-\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\alpha}-1}\log\frac{d\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}{d\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}\right)\right]\right]

(D.12)





Proof.


To simplify notations in this proof, unless otherwise specified, {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}θ{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta} indicates a random variable distributed according to \widehat{\prob}(\cdot\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X})ℙ^(⋅|X)\widehat{\prob}(\cdot\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}).

\displaystyle\ex_{#1}\left[-\log\ex_{#1}\left[\left(\frac{d\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}{d\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot)}\right)^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}}\right]\right]\leq𝔼#​1⁡[−log⁡𝔼#​1⁡[(dℙ#​1(⋅|θ)d​ℙ#​1​(⋅))ρ]]≤\displaystyle\ex_{#1}\left[-\log\ex_{#1}\left[\left(\frac{d\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}{d\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot)}\right)^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}}\right]\right]\leq
\displaystyle\ex_{#1}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}\log\frac{d\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot)}{d\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}\right]+\ex_{#1}\left[{\mathrm{KL}}\!(\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X})\,\|\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi})\right]\,.𝔼#​1[ρlogd​ℙ#​1​(⋅)dℙ#​1(⋅|θ)]+𝔼#​1[KL(ℙ#​1(⋅|X)∥π)].\displaystyle\ex_{#1}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}\log\frac{d\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot)}{d\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}\right]+\ex_{#1}\left[{\mathrm{KL}}\!(\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X})\,\|\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi})\right]\,.

(D.13)



The  LHS (LHS) is the quantity we want to bound. We now only need to bound the RHS.
Making {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta}θ0∈Θ{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta} appear in the bound, we have







\displaystyle\ex_{#1}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}\log\frac{d\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot)}{d\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}\right]+\ex_{#1}\left[{\mathrm{KL}}\!(\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X})\,\|\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi})\right]𝔼#​1[ρlogd​ℙ#​1​(⋅)dℙ#​1(⋅|θ)]+𝔼#​1[KL(ℙ#​1(⋅|X)∥π)]\displaystyle\ex_{#1}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}\log\frac{d\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot)}{d\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}\right]+\ex_{#1}\left[{\mathrm{KL}}\!(\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X})\,\|\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi})\right]

(D.14)





\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}\ex_{#1}\left[\log\frac{d\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot)}{d\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}\right]+\ex_{#1}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}\log\frac{d\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}{d\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}\right]+\ex_{#1}\left[{\mathrm{KL}}\!(\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X})\,\|\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi})\right]=ρ𝔼#​1[logd​ℙ#​1​(⋅)dℙ#​1(⋅|θ0)]+𝔼#​1[ρlogdℙ#​1(⋅|θ0)dℙ#​1(⋅|θ)]+𝔼#​1[KL(ℙ#​1(⋅|X)∥π)]\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}\ex_{#1}\left[\log\frac{d\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot)}{d\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}\right]+\ex_{#1}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}\log\frac{d\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}})}{d\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})}\right]+\ex_{#1}\left[{\mathrm{KL}}\!(\prob_{\!#1}(\cdot\nonscript\>|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X})\,\|\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi})\right]

D.3 ICL setting




Let us now re-introduce the ICL setting from Section 3.1 along with the detailed assumptions.




\lVert\cdot\rVert∥⋅∥\lVert\cdot\rVert denotes the Euclidean norm on {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}^{d}ℝd{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}^{d} for any d\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}d∈ℕd\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}.

D.4 Task Selection Bound for ICL




We begin with a discretization argument and first we generalize the bracketing numbers to the non-i.i.d. case.
This definition generalizes the bracketing numbers used in Barron et al. (1999); Zhang (2003); Zhang (2006) to the non-i.i.d case and the following result generalises the results of Zhang (2006) to the non-i.i.d. case.

We can now state our main result for ICL.

Proof.


This is a direct consequence of Proposition D.1 combined with Lemma D.4 with {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lambda}=1+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}λ=1+ρ{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lambda}=1+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho} and bounding {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}\right)^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}}\leq 1π​(θ∉Θ′)μ≤1{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\notin{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Theta^{\prime}}\right)^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}}\leq 1.
∎





A few comments are in order.
The first term of Eq. D.69 captures how much the prior {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}π{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi} covers the reference task {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}}θ0{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}}. When {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}^{*}θ0=θ∗{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}^{*}, this term thus quantifies how well the prior covers the true task {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}^{*}θ∗{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}^{*}. When {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}}θ0{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}} is inside the support of {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}π{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}, this term is vanishing as {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}T{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T} grows large, see the next results below.




The second term of Eq. D.70 captures how well the reference task {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}}θ0{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}} approximates the true task {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}^{*}θ∗{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}^{*}.
When {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}^{*}θ0=θ∗{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}_{0}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}^{*}, the term of Eq. D.70 is 0. Otherwise, consider the case the KL will typically be of order {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}T{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T} so that this term is \bigoh(1)𝒪⁡(1)\bigoh(1): it represents the best ICL error one can hope for when the true task {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}^{*}θ∗{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}^{*} is not in the support of the prior {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}π{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\pi}.







D.5 Laplace Approximation




We will make use of the following version of the Laplace approximation, see Wong (2001, Chap. 9, Thm. 3) for a proof.



Lemma D.5 (Laplace approximation).


Let \muμ\mu be a probability measure on \mathbb{R}^{d}ℝd\mathbb{R}^{d} with density g:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to[0,\infty)g:ℝd→[0,∞)g:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to[0,\infty). Fix x^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}x∗∈ℝdx^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{d} such that ggg is continuous at x^{*}x∗x^{*} and g(x^{*})>0g⁡(x∗)>0g(x^{*})>0.
Then, as \varepsilon\to 0ε→0\varepsilon\to 0,






\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp\!\Bigl(-\tfrac{1}{2\varepsilon}\,\|x-x^{*}\|\Bigr)\,g(x)\,dx,\;=\;g(x^{*})\,C\,\varepsilon^{d}\;+\;o(\varepsilon^{d}).∫ℝdexp⁡(−12​ε​‖x−x∗‖)​g​(x)​𝑑x,=g⁡(x∗)​C​εd+o⁡(εd).\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp\!\Bigl(-\tfrac{1}{2\varepsilon}\,\|x-x^{*}\|\Bigr)\,g(x)\,dx,\;=\;g(x^{*})\,C\,\varepsilon^{d}\;+\;o(\varepsilon^{d}).




where C\;\coloneqq\;\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp\!\Bigl(-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\|y\|\Bigr)\,dy\in(0,\infty)C≔∫ℝdexp⁡(−12​‖y‖)​𝑑y∈(0,∞)C\;\coloneqq\;\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp\!\Bigl(-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\|y\|\Bigr)\,dy\in(0,\infty).

Appendix E Generalization bounds





E.1 Moment bounds for general functions




In this subsection, we generalize the heavy-tail concentration results of Li and Liu (2024a) to allow for non-i.i.d. data.
This section can also be seen as extending concentration results for dependent sequences to the case where the function of interest does not necessarily admit bounded differences but only bounded moments.
In particular, Lemma E.1 extends the coupling argument of Chazottes et al. (2007) to our setting, in particular not requiring bounded differences but only bounded moments. Indeed, for this, we replace the total variation distance by the Wasserstein-1 distance. It can also be seen as an extension of the bounded differences result of Kontorovich and Ramanan (2008) to our setting (see Mohri and Rostamizadeh (2010) for a presentation of the results of Kontorovich and Ramanan (2008) in a setting closer to ours).
Moreover, note that even the handling of the subGaussian increments is much more trickier than in Kontorovich (2014), since we have to carefully apply a convex domination argument to handle the conditional dependence.
The main result of this section is Theorem E.1, which is of independent interest.




As in the previous section, \lVert\cdot\rVert∥⋅∥\lVert\cdot\rVert denotes the Euclidean norm on {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}^{d}ℝd{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}^{d} for any d\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}d∈ℕd\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}.




At multiple places, we will use the Wasserstein-1 distance§§
              §
              
              
              
              
              
              
              
            This is a slight abuse of terminology, since the Wasserstein-1 distance is usually defined for metric spaces, while we only assume \rhoρ\rho to be a cost function. However, this slight abuse of terminology will not cause any confusion in the following.
with respect to a cost function \rho\colon\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{Z}\to[0,\infty)ρ:𝒵×𝒵→[0,∞)\rho\colon\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{Z}\to[0,\infty), defined as






W_{#1}(\mu,\nu):=\inf_{\pi\in\Pi(\mu,\nu)}\int\rho(z,z^{\prime})d\pi(z,z^{\prime}),W#​1​(μ,ν):=infπ∈Π⁡(μ,ν)∫ρ⁡(z,z′)​𝑑π​(z,z′),W_{#1}(\mu,\nu):=\inf_{\pi\in\Pi(\mu,\nu)}\int\rho(z,z^{\prime})d\pi(z,z^{\prime}),

(E.1)



where \Pi(\mu,\nu)Π⁡(μ,ν)\Pi(\mu,\nu) is the set of couplings of \muμ\mu and \nuν\nu. We refer to the textbook Villani (2008) for more details.



Lemma E.1.


Consider \mathcal{Z}𝒵\mathcal{Z} measurable space. (E.3)





With W_{#1}(\cdot,\cdot)W#​1​(⋅,⋅)W_{#1}(\cdot,\cdot) the Wasserstein-1 distance with respect to \rho_{j}ρj\rho_{j}, define, for i<ji<ji<j,






\delta_{i,j}(z_{1:i},z_{i}^{\prime})=W_{#1}(\text{Law}(Z_{j}\mid Z_{1:i}=z_{1:i}),\text{Law}(Z_{j}\mid Z_{1:i-1}=z_{1:i-1},Z_{i}=z_{i}^{\prime})).δi,j(z1:i,zi′)=W#​1(Law(Zj∣Z1:i=z1:i),Law(Zj∣Z1:i−1=z1:i−1,Zi=zi′)).\delta_{i,j}(z_{1:i},z_{i}^{\prime})=W_{#1}(\text{Law}(Z_{j}\mid Z_{1:i}=z_{1:i}),\text{Law}(Z_{j}\mid Z_{1:i-1}=z_{1:i-1},Z_{i}=z_{i}^{\prime})).

\lvert\ex[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}(Z_{1:m})\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}\mathcal{F}_{i}]-\ex[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}(Z_{1:i-1},Z_{i}^{\prime},Z_{i+1:m})\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}\mathcal{F}_{i-1},Z_{i}^{\prime}]\rvert\leq L_{i}\rho_{i}(Z_{i},Z_{i}^{\prime})+\sum_{j=i+1}^{m}L_{j}\delta_{i,j}(Z_{1:i},Z_{i}^{\prime})|𝔼[g(Z1:m)|ℱi]−𝔼[g(Z1:i−1,Zi′,Zi+1:m)|ℱi−1,Zi′]|≤Liρi(Zi,Zi′)+∑j=i+1mLjδi,j(Z1:i,Zi′)\lvert\ex[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}(Z_{1:m})\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}\mathcal{F}_{i}]-\ex[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}(Z_{1:i-1},Z_{i}^{\prime},Z_{i+1:m})\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}\mathcal{F}_{i-1},Z_{i}^{\prime}]\rvert\leq L_{i}\rho_{i}(Z_{i},Z_{i}^{\prime})+\sum_{j=i+1}^{m}L_{j}\delta_{i,j}(Z_{1:i},Z_{i}^{\prime})

\displaystyle:={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}(Z_{1:(i-1)},u^{\prime},z_{i+1:m}).:=g(Z1:(i−1),u′,zi+1:m).\displaystyle:={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}(Z_{1:(i-1)},u^{\prime},z_{i+1:m}).

(E.7)





Denote Z_{(i+1):m}\sim\text{Law}(Z_{(i+1):m}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1},Z_{i}=u)Z(i+1):m∼Law(Z(i+1):m∣ℱi−1,Zi=u)Z_{(i+1):m}\sim\text{Law}(Z_{(i+1):m}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1},Z_{i}=u) and
Z_{(i+1):m}^{\prime}\sim\text{Law}(Z_{(i+1):m}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1},Z_{i}=u^{\prime})Z(i+1):m′∼Law(Z(i+1):m∣ℱi−1,Zi=u′)Z_{(i+1):m}^{\prime}\sim\text{Law}(Z_{(i+1):m}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1},Z_{i}=u^{\prime}). We decompose







\displaystyle\lvert\ex[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}(Z_{1:m})]-\ex[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}(Z_{1:(i-1)},Z_{i:m}^{\prime})]\rvert|𝔼[g(Z1:m)]−𝔼[g(Z1:(i−1),Zi:m′)]|\displaystyle\lvert\ex[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}(Z_{1:m})]-\ex[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}(Z_{1:(i-1)},Z_{i:m}^{\prime})]\rvert

(E.8)





\displaystyle=\big|\ex[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}(Z_{(i+1):m})]-\ex[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):m}^{\prime})]\big|=|𝔼[ψ(Z(i+1):m)]−𝔼[ψ′(Z(i+1):m′)]|\displaystyle=\big|\ex[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}(Z_{(i+1):m})]-\ex[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):m}^{\prime})]\big|

(E.9)





\displaystyle\leq\ex\left[\big|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}(Z_{(i+1):m})-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):m})\big|\right]+\Big|\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):m})\right]-\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):m}^{\prime})\right]\Big|.≤𝔼[|ψ(Z(i+1):m)−ψ′(Z(i+1):m)|]+|𝔼[ψ′(Z(i+1):m)]−𝔼[ψ′(Z(i+1):m′)]|.\displaystyle\leq\ex\left[\big|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}(Z_{(i+1):m})-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):m})\big|\right]+\Big|\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):m})\right]-\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):m}^{\prime})\right]\Big|.

(E.10)





We bound the two terms separately.




By the coordinate-wise Lipschitz condition at iii,






\ex_{#1}\left[|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}(Z_{(i+1):m})-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):m})|\right]\leq L_{i}\rho_{i}(u,u^{\prime})=L_{i}\rho_{i}(Z_{i},Z_{i}^{\prime}).𝔼#​1[|ψ(Z(i+1):m)−ψ′(Z(i+1):m)|]≤Liρi(u,u′)=Liρi(Zi,Zi′).\ex_{#1}\left[|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}(Z_{(i+1):m})-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):m})|\right]\leq L_{i}\rho_{i}(u,u^{\prime})=L_{i}\rho_{i}(Z_{i},Z_{i}^{\prime}).

(E.11)





We write the following telescoping decomposition:






\displaystyle\Big|\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):m})\right]-\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):m}^{\prime})\right]\Big||𝔼[ψ′(Z(i+1):m)]−𝔼[ψ′(Z(i+1):m′)]|\displaystyle\Big|\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):m})\right]-\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):m}^{\prime})\right]\Big|
\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=i}^{m-1}\Big|\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):j}^{\prime},Z_{(j+1):m})\right]-\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):(j+1)}^{\prime},Z_{(j+1):m})\right]\Big|\,.≤∑j=im−1|𝔼[ψ′(Z(i+1):j′,Z(j+1):m)]−𝔼[ψ′(Z(i+1):(j+1)′,Z(j+1):m)]|.\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=i}^{m-1}\Big|\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):j}^{\prime},Z_{(j+1):m})\right]-\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):(j+1)}^{\prime},Z_{(j+1):m})\right]\Big|\,.

(E.12)





By the definition of the Wasserstein-1 distance, there exists a coupling of (Z_{j+1},Z_{j+1}^{\prime})(Zj+1,Zj+1′)(Z_{j+1},Z_{j+1}^{\prime}) such that






\ex\left[\rho_{j+1}(Z_{j+1},Z_{j+1}^{\prime})\nonscript\,\middle|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}\mathcal{F}_{i},Z_{i}^{\prime}\right]=W_{#1}(\text{Law}(Z_{j+1}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i}),\text{Law}(Z_{j+1}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1},Z_{i}^{\prime}))\leq\delta_{i,j+1}(Z_{1:i-1},Z_{i}^{\prime})\,.𝔼[ρj+1(Zj+1,Zj+1′)|ℱi,Zi′]=W#​1(Law(Zj+1∣ℱi),Law(Zj+1∣ℱi−1,Zi′))≤δi,j+1(Z1:i−1,Zi′).\ex\left[\rho_{j+1}(Z_{j+1},Z_{j+1}^{\prime})\nonscript\,\middle|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}\mathcal{F}_{i},Z_{i}^{\prime}\right]=W_{#1}(\text{Law}(Z_{j+1}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i}),\text{Law}(Z_{j+1}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1},Z_{i}^{\prime}))\leq\delta_{i,j+1}(Z_{1:i-1},Z_{i}^{\prime})\,.

(E.13)





We obtain a bound on the increment at coordinate jjj by combining the coupling with the coordinate-wise Lipschitz condition at jjj:







\displaystyle\Big|\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):j}^{\prime},Z_{(j+1):m})\right]-\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):(j+1)}^{\prime},Z_{(j+1):m})\right]\Big||𝔼[ψ′(Z(i+1):j′,Z(j+1):m)]−𝔼[ψ′(Z(i+1):(j+1)′,Z(j+1):m)]|\displaystyle\Big|\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):j}^{\prime},Z_{(j+1):m})\right]-\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):(j+1)}^{\prime},Z_{(j+1):m})\right]\Big|

(E.14)





\displaystyle\leq\ex\left[\big|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):j}^{\prime},Z_{(j+1):m})-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):(j+1)}^{\prime},Z_{(j+1):m})\big|\right]≤𝔼[|ψ′(Z(i+1):j′,Z(j+1):m)−ψ′(Z(i+1):(j+1)′,Z(j+1):m)|]\displaystyle\leq\ex\left[\big|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):j}^{\prime},Z_{(j+1):m})-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):(j+1)}^{\prime},Z_{(j+1):m})\big|\right]

(E.15)





\displaystyle\leq L_{j+1}\ex\left[\rho_{j+1}(Z_{j+1},Z_{j+1}^{\prime})\right]≤Lj+1​𝔼⁡[ρj+1​(Zj+1,Zj+1′)]\displaystyle\leq L_{j+1}\ex\left[\rho_{j+1}(Z_{j+1},Z_{j+1}^{\prime})\right]

(E.16)





\displaystyle=L_{j+1}W_{#1}(\text{Law}(Z_{j+1}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i}),\text{Law}(Z_{j+1}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1},Z_{i}^{\prime}))=L_{j+1}\delta_{i,j+1}(Z_{1:i},Z_{i}^{\prime})\,.=Lj+1W#​1(Law(Zj+1∣ℱi),Law(Zj+1∣ℱi−1,Zi′))=Lj+1δi,j+1(Z1:i,Zi′).\displaystyle=L_{j+1}W_{#1}(\text{Law}(Z_{j+1}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i}),\text{Law}(Z_{j+1}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1},Z_{i}^{\prime}))=L_{j+1}\delta_{i,j+1}(Z_{1:i},Z_{i}^{\prime})\,.

(E.17)





Combining the above estimates gives






\displaystyle\Big|\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):m})\right]-\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):m}^{\prime})\right]\Big||𝔼[ψ′(Z(i+1):m)]−𝔼[ψ′(Z(i+1):m′)]|\displaystyle\Big|\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):m})\right]-\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\psi}^{\prime}(Z_{(i+1):m}^{\prime})\right]\Big|
\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=i}^{m-1}L_{j+1}\delta_{i,j+1}(Z_{1:i},Z_{i}^{\prime})\,.≤∑j=im−1Lj+1δi,j+1(Z1:i,Zi′).\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=i}^{m-1}L_{j+1}\delta_{i,j+1}(Z_{1:i},Z_{i}^{\prime})\,.

(E.18)



which yields the desired result.
∎





We now state a classic convex domination lemma which is a slight variant of Ledoux and Talagrand (2013, Lem. 4.6).



Lemma E.2 (Convex domination).


Consider X,ZX,ZX,Z a zero-mean symmetric random variables such that






\prob(|X|>t)\leq C\prob(|Z|>t)\,,ℙ⁡(|X|>t)≤C​ℙ⁡(|Z|>t),\prob(|X|>t)\leq C\prob(|Z|>t)\,,

(E.19)



for some C>0C>0C>0 and all t>0t>0t>0.




Then, for any convex function h\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}h:ℝ→ℝh\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}},






\ex[h(X)]\leq\ex[h(CZ)]\,.𝔼⁡[h⁡(X)]≤𝔼⁡[h⁡(CZ)].\ex[h(X)]\leq\ex[h(CZ)]\,.

(E.20)





Proof.


Let \delta\sim\mathrm{Bernoulli}(1/C)δ∼Bernoulli⁡(1/C)\delta\sim\mathrm{Bernoulli}(1/C) be independent of (X,Z)(X,Z)(X,Z). Then, for all t>0t>0t>0,
\prob(|Z|>t)\geq\tfrac{1}{C}\,\prob(|X|>t)=\prob(|\delta X|>t).ℙ⁡(|Z|>t)≥1C​ℙ⁡(|X|>t)=ℙ⁡(|δ​X|>t).\prob(|Z|>t)\geq\tfrac{1}{C}\,\prob(|X|>t)=\prob(|\delta X|>t).
Hence |\delta X||δ​X||\delta X| is stochastically dominated by |Z||Z||Z| and we may construct a coupling such that






|\delta X|\leq|Z|\qquad\text{a.s.}|δ​X|≤|Z|a.s.|\delta X|\leq|Z|\qquad\text{a.s.}

(E.21)





Since XXX is symmetric, we may write in distribution X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}\,|X|X=dε​|X|X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}\,|X| where {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}ε{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon} is a Rademacher variable independent of |X||X||X|. Likewise, Z\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}^{\prime}\,|Z|Z=dε′​|Z|Z\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}^{\prime}\,|Z| with an independent Rademacher {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}^{\prime}ε′{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}^{\prime}.




Condition on (\delta,X,Z)(δ,X,Z)(\delta,X,Z) and define






\Phi(a)\;:=\;\ex\big[\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}\!\big(a\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}\,|Z|\big)\,\bigm|\,\delta,X,Z\big]\,,\qquad a\in[-1,1].Φ(a):=𝔼[h(aε|Z|)|δ,X,Z],a∈[−1,1].\Phi(a)\;:=\;\ex\big[\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}\!\big(a\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}\,|Z|\big)\,\bigm|\,\delta,X,Z\big]\,,\qquad a\in[-1,1].

(E.22)



The map a\mapsto\Phi(a)a↦Φ⁡(a)a\mapsto\Phi(a) is convex (as an average of convex functions). By convexity, its maximum on [-1,1][−1,1][-1,1] is attained at an extreme point \{-1,1\}{−1,1}\{-1,1\}. On the coupling where (E.21) holds, define






a\;:=\;\begin{cases}\dfrac{\delta\lvert X\rvert}{|Z|},&\text{if }Z\neq 0,\\[6.0pt]
0,&\text{if }Z=0,\end{cases}a:={δ​|X||Z|,if ​Z≠0,0,if ​Z=0,a\;:=\;\begin{cases}\dfrac{\delta\lvert X\rvert}{|Z|},&\text{if }Z\neq 0,\\[6.0pt]
0,&\text{if }Z=0,\end{cases}

(E.23)



so that a\in[-1,1]a∈[−1,1]a\in[-1,1] almost surely thanks to |X|\leq|\delta Z||X|≤|δ​Z||X|\leq|\delta Z|. Therefore,






\displaystyle\ex\big[\,h\!\big({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}\,|X|\,\delta\big)\,\bigm|\,\delta,X,Z\big]\;=\;\Phi(a)\;\leq\;\max\{\Phi(-1),\Phi(1)\}\;=\;\ex\big[\,h\!\big({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}\,|Z|\big)\,\bigm|\,\delta,|X|,Z\big].𝔼[h(ε|X|δ)|δ,X,Z]=Φ(a)≤max{Φ(−1),Φ(1)}=𝔼[h(ε|Z|)|δ,|X|,Z].\displaystyle\ex\big[\,h\!\big({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}\,|X|\,\delta\big)\,\bigm|\,\delta,X,Z\big]\;=\;\Phi(a)\;\leq\;\max\{\Phi(-1),\Phi(1)\}\;=\;\ex\big[\,h\!\big({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}\,|Z|\big)\,\bigm|\,\delta,|X|,Z\big].

(E.24)



Taking expectations and using X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}|X|X=dε​|X|X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}|X| and Z\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}|Z|Z=dε​|Z|Z\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}|Z|,






\ex[h\!\big(\delta X\big)]\;\leq\;\ex[h(Z)].𝔼⁡[h⁡(δ​X)]≤𝔼⁡[h⁡(Z)].\ex[h\!\big(\delta X\big)]\;\leq\;\ex[h(Z)].

(E.25)





Since hhh is convex and \ex[\delta\mid X,Z]=1/C𝔼[δ∣X,Z]=1/C\ex[\delta\mid X,Z]=1/C, we have, by Jensen’s inequality,






\ex[h\!\big(X/C\big)]\;=\;\ex\big[\,h\!\big(\ex[\delta X\mid X,Z]\big)]\;\leq\;\ex\big[\,\ex[h(\delta X)\mid X,Z]\,]\;=\;\ex[h(\delta X)]\leq\ex[h(Z)]\,,𝔼[h(X/C)]=𝔼[h(𝔼[δX∣X,Z])]≤𝔼[𝔼[h(δX)∣X,Z]]=𝔼[h(δX)]≤𝔼[h(Z)],\ex[h\!\big(X/C\big)]\;=\;\ex\big[\,h\!\big(\ex[\delta X\mid X,Z]\big)]\;\leq\;\ex\big[\,\ex[h(\delta X)\mid X,Z]\,]\;=\;\ex[h(\delta X)]\leq\ex[h(Z)]\,,

(E.26)



Finally, apply the previous inequality with the convex function u\mapsto h(Cu)u↦h⁡(C​u)u\mapsto h(Cu) to obtain






\ex[h(X)]\;=\;\ex[h\!\big(C\cdot(X/C)\big)]\;\leq\;\ex[h\!\big(CZ\big)].𝔼⁡[h⁡(X)]=𝔼⁡[h⁡(C⋅(X/C))]≤𝔼⁡[h⁡(CZ)].\ex[h(X)]\;=\;\ex[h\!\big(C\cdot(X/C)\big)]\;\leq\;\ex[h\!\big(CZ\big)].




This is exactly the desired bound.




∎





We now state a fact of subGaussian random variables, which can be found in Wainwright (2019, Thm. 2.6) for instance.



Lemma E.3 (Convex domination).


Consider XXX a zero-mean real-valued \sigma^{2}σ2\sigma^{2}-sub-Gaussian random variable,
which is, in addition, symmetric, i.e., X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}-XX=d−XX\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}-X.
Then, for Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})Z∼𝒩⁡(0,σ2)Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2}),






\prob(|X|>t)\leq 8\prob(|Z|>t)\,.ℙ⁡(|X|>t)≤8​ℙ⁡(|Z|>t).\prob(|X|>t)\leq 8\prob(|Z|>t)\,.

(E.27)





Lemma E.4 (Causal symmetrization).


Let m\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}m∈ℕm\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}} and (\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{A})(𝒵,𝒜)(\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{A}) be a standard Borel measurable space. Let Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{m}Z1,…,ZmZ_{1},\ldots,Z_{m} be \mathcal{Z}𝒵\mathcal{Z}-valued random with natural filtration (\mathcal{F}_{i})_{i=0,\dots,m}(ℱi)i=0,…,m(\mathcal{F}_{i})_{i=0,\dots,m}
Let h\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}h:ℝ→ℝh\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}} be convex.




Consider {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}\colon\mathcal{Z}^{m}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}g:𝒵m→ℝ{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}\colon\mathcal{Z}^{m}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}} be measurable. Set S:={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{m})S:=g⁡(Z1,…,Zm)S:={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{m}).
For each i\in\{1,\ldots,m\}i∈{1,…,m}i\in\{1,\ldots,m\}, assume there exists a conditionally independent resample






Z_{i}^{\prime}\sim\mathrm{Law}(Z_{i}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}),\quad Z_{i}^{\prime}\perp\!\!\!\perp Z_{i}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}.Zi′∼Law(Zi∣ℱi−1),Zi′⟂⟂Zi∣ℱi−1.Z_{i}^{\prime}\sim\mathrm{Law}(Z_{i}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}),\quad Z_{i}^{\prime}\perp\!\!\!\perp Z_{i}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}.

(E.28)



Let {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{1:m},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{1:m}^{\prime}ε1:m,ε1:m′{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{1:m},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{1:m}^{\prime} be independent Rademacher variables, independent of all Z,Z^{\prime}Z,Z′Z,Z^{\prime} and \mathcal{F}_{m}ℱm\mathcal{F}_{m}.




Assume there exist measurable functions c_{i}\colon\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{Z}\to[0,\infty)ci:𝒵×𝒵→[0,∞)c_{i}\colon\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{Z}\to[0,\infty), d_{i}:\mathcal{Z}\to[0,\infty)di:𝒵→[0,∞)d_{i}:\mathcal{Z}\to[0,\infty) and J\subset\left\{1,\ldots,m\right\}J⊂{1,…,m}J\subset\left\{1,\ldots,m\right\} such that, the following conditions hold:



(i) 


For any iii, there exists j(i)\in Jj⁡(i)∈Jj(i)\in J, such that, for any z_{1:i-1}\in\mathcal{Z}^{i-1}z1:i−1∈𝒵i−1z_{1:i-1}\in\mathcal{Z}^{i-1} and z_{i},z_{i}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}zi,zi′∈𝒵z_{i},z_{i}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z},






\left\lvert\ex\left[S\nonscript\,\middle|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:i}=z_{1:i}\right]-\ex[S\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:i-1}=z_{1:i-1},Z_{i}=z_{i}^{\prime}]\right\rvert\leq c_{i}(z_{i},z_{i}^{\prime})+d_{i}(z_{j(i)})\one\{i\not\in J\}\,.|𝔼[S|Z1:i=z1:i]−𝔼[S|Z1:i−1=z1:i−1,Zi=zi′]|≤ci(zi,zi′)+di(zj⁡(i))𝟙{i∉J}.\left\lvert\ex\left[S\nonscript\,\middle|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:i}=z_{1:i}\right]-\ex[S\nonscript\,|\nonscript\,\mathopen{}Z_{1:i-1}=z_{1:i-1},Z_{i}=z_{i}^{\prime}]\right\rvert\leq c_{i}(z_{i},z_{i}^{\prime})+d_{i}(z_{j(i)})\one\{i\not\in J\}\,.

(E.29)





(ii) 


For any i\notin Ji∉Ji\notin J, {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{i}c_{i}(Z_{i},Z_{i}^{\prime})εi​ci​(Zi,Zi′){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{i}c_{i}(Z_{i},Z_{i}^{\prime}) is \sigma_{i}^{2}σi2\sigma_{i}^{2}-sub-Gaussian conditionally on \mathcal{F}_{i-1}ℱi−1\mathcal{F}_{i-1}.




(iii) 


For any j\in Jj∈Jj\in J, Z_{j}ZjZ_{j} is independent of \mathcal{F}_{j-1}ℱj−1\mathcal{F}_{j-1}.





Then, there are Gausssian random variables G_{j},G_{j}^{\prime}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,8\sigma_{j}^{2})Gj,Gj′∼𝒩⁡(0,8​σj2)G_{j},G_{j}^{\prime}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,8\sigma_{j}^{2}) independent and independent of all Z,Z^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon},\mathcal{F}_{m}Z,Z′,ε,ℱmZ,Z^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon},\mathcal{F}_{m} such that






\ex[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}(S-\ex[S])]\leq\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}\left(\sum_{i\notin J}\sym_{#1}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{i}\left(\lvert G_{i}\rvert+d_{i}(Z_{j(i)})\right)\right)+\sum_{j\in J}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{j}c_{j}(Z_{j},Z_{j}^{\prime})\right)\right],𝔼⁡[h⁡(S−𝔼⁡[S])]≤𝔼⁡[h⁡(∑i∉JSym#​1⁡(εi​(|Gi|+di​(Zj⁡(i))))+∑j∈Jεj​cj​(Zj,Zj′))],\ex[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}(S-\ex[S])]\leq\ex\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}\left(\sum_{i\notin J}\sym_{#1}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{i}\left(\lvert G_{i}\rvert+d_{i}(Z_{j(i)})\right)\right)+\sum_{j\in J}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}_{j}c_{j}(Z_{j},Z_{j}^{\prime})\right)\right],

(E.30)



where we use the notation:

Theorem E.1 (Causal symmetrization).


Let m\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}}m∈ℕm\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{N}} and (\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{A})(𝒵,𝒜)(\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{A}) be a standard Borel measurable space. (E.70)



Set S:={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{m})S:=g⁡(Z1,…,Zm)S:={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}g}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{m}) and

E.2 Technical lemmas




We will make use of the following elementary lemma.



Lemma E.5.


Let ZZZ be a real-valued random variable. Assume there exist c\geq 1c≥1c\geq 1, f,g\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}}_{+}f,g:ℝ→ℝ+f,g\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}}_{+} non-decreasing and p\geq 2p≥2p\geq 2 integer such that, for any integer q\in[2,p]q∈[2,p]q\in[2,p],






\ex[|Z|^{q}]^{1/q}\leq f(q)+c^{1/q}g(q)𝔼⁡[|Z|q]1/q≤f⁡(q)+c1/q​g​(q)\ex[|Z|^{q}]^{1/q}\leq f(q)+c^{1/q}g(q)

(E.77)



Then, for any \delta\in(0,e^{-2}]δ∈(0,e−2]\delta\in(0,e^{-2}], with probability at least 1-\delta1−δ1-\delta,






\left\lvert Z\right\rvert\leq\begin{cases}ef\left(\log(1/\delta)+1\right)+g(\log(1/\delta)+1)e&\text{if }\delta\geq ce^{-p}\\
\frac{f(p)+c^{1/p}g(p)}{\delta^{1/p}}&\text{if }\delta<ce^{-p}\,.\end{cases}|Z|≤{e​f​(log⁡(1/δ)+1)+g⁡(log⁡(1/δ)+1)​eif ​δ≥c​e−pf⁡(p)+c1/p​g​(p)δ1/pif ​δ<c​e−p.\left\lvert Z\right\rvert\leq\begin{cases}ef\left(\log(1/\delta)+1\right)+g(\log(1/\delta)+1)e&\text{if }\delta\geq ce^{-p}\\
\frac{f(p)+c^{1/p}g(p)}{\delta^{1/p}}&\text{if }\delta<ce^{-p}\,.\end{cases}

(E.78)





Proof.


By Markov’s inequality, for any integer q\in[2,p]q∈[2,p]q\in[2,p],






\prob(|Z|\geq t)\leq\frac{\ex[|Z|^{q}]}{t^{q}}\leq\left(\frac{f(q)+c^{1/q}g(q)}{t}\right)^{q}\,.ℙ⁡(|Z|≥t)≤𝔼⁡[|Z|q]tq≤(f⁡(q)+c1/q​g​(q)t)q.\prob(|Z|\geq t)\leq\frac{\ex[|Z|^{q}]}{t^{q}}\leq\left(\frac{f(q)+c^{1/q}g(q)}{t}\right)^{q}\,.

(E.79)



Setting the right-hand side to \deltaδ\delta and solving for ttt gives






t=\frac{f(q)+c^{1/q}g(q)}{\delta^{1/q}}\,,t=f⁡(q)+c1/q​g​(q)δ1/q,t=\frac{f(q)+c^{1/q}g(q)}{\delta^{1/q}}\,,

(E.80)





If \delta<ce^{-p}δ<c​e−p\delta<ce^{-p}, we can take q=pq=pq=p to obtain the second case of the result. If \delta\geq ce^{-p}δ≥c​e−p\delta\geq ce^{-p}, we take qqq the smallest integer such that q\geq\log(c/\delta)q≥log⁡(c/δ)q\geq\log(c/\delta). Note that qqq is in [2,p][2,p][2,p] and q\leq\log(c/\delta)+2q≤log⁡(c/δ)+2q\leq\log(c/\delta)+2.




Since c\geq 1c≥1c\geq 1 and \delta\leq 1δ≤1\delta\leq 1, we have \log(c/\delta)\geq 0log⁡(c/δ)≥0\log(c/\delta)\geq 0 and thus \left(\frac{c}{\delta}\right)^{1/q}\leq\left(\frac{c}{\delta}\right)^{1/\log(c/\delta)}=e(cδ)1/q≤(cδ)1/log⁡(c/δ)=e\left(\frac{c}{\delta}\right)^{1/q}\leq\left(\frac{c}{\delta}\right)^{1/\log(c/\delta)}=e.
Plugging this into Eq. E.80 gives the bound in the first case.
∎





We state the following lemma about sub-Gaussian random variables that will be useful later.



Lemma E.6.


Let X\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}}^{m}X∈ℝmX\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}}^{m} be a \sigma^{2}σ2\sigma^{2}-sub-Gaussian random variable, i.e., for any \lambda>0λ>0\lambda>0,






\log\ex[e^{\lambda\lVert X-\ex[X]\rVert^{2}}]\leq\frac{\sigma^{2}\lambda^{2}}{2}\,.log⁡𝔼⁡[eλ​∥X−𝔼⁡[X]∥2]≤σ2​λ22.\log\ex[e^{\lambda\lVert X-\ex[X]\rVert^{2}}]\leq\frac{\sigma^{2}\lambda^{2}}{2}\,.

(E.81)



Then, for X^{\prime}X′X^{\prime} an iid copy of XXX and {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}ε{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon} a Rademacher random variable independent of X,X^{\prime}X,X′X,X^{\prime}, the random variable {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}\lVert X-X^{\prime}\rVertε​∥X−X′∥{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}\lVert X-X^{\prime}\rVert is sub-Gaussian with parameter at most 4\sigma^{2}4​σ24\sigma^{2}.




Proof.


Since Z\coloneqq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}\lVert X-X^{\prime}\rVertZ≔ε⁡∥X−X′∥Z\coloneqq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\varepsilon}\lVert X-X^{\prime}\rVert is symmetric, it suffices to bound Z^{2}Z2Z^{2} as






Z^{2}=\lVert X-X^{\prime}\rVert^{2}\leq 2\lVert X-\ex[X]\rVert^{2}+2\lVert X^{\prime}-\ex[X]\rVert^{2}\,,Z2=∥X−X′∥2≤2​∥X−𝔼⁡[X]∥2+2​∥X′−𝔼⁡[X]∥2,Z^{2}=\lVert X-X^{\prime}\rVert^{2}\leq 2\lVert X-\ex[X]\rVert^{2}+2\lVert X^{\prime}-\ex[X]\rVert^{2}\,,

(E.82)



by Young’s inequality. Using the independence of XXX and X^{\prime}X′X^{\prime} yields the result.
∎





We will require the following chaining lemma for processes with L^{p}LpL^{p}-Lipschitz increments.
This result is a variant of the famous Dudley’s entropy integral bound for sub-Gaussian processes, adapted to the L^{p}LpL^{p}-Lipschitz setting.




This lemma is a direct consequence of the general chaining theory of Talagrand (2022) (see Talagrand (2022, Thm. B.2.3) with \phi(x)=x^{p}ϕ⁡(x)=xp\phi(x)=x^{p}). Let us also mention Dirksen (2015) refined these ideas in the context of subpexponential processes while Latała and Tkocz (2015) further developed these tools for processes with heavier tails but still admitting a control over all moments. In our setting, the increments are assumed to be controlled only in L^{p}LpL^{p}, which requires a different treatment of the maximal inequalities at each scale.



Lemma E.7 (Dudley–type entropy integral under L^{p}LpL^{p} increments).


Let (X_{t})_{t\in T}(Xt)t∈T(X_{t})_{t\in T} be a real-valued process indexed by a pseudometric space (T,d)(T,d)(T,d).
Assume TTT is totally bounded with diameter \Delta:=\mathrm{diam}_{d}(T)\in(0,\infty)Δ:=diamd​(T)∈(0,∞)\Delta:=\mathrm{diam}_{d}(T)\in(0,\infty) and that for some p>1p>1p>1 and L>0L>0L>0,






\|X_{t}-X_{s}\|_{p}\;\leq\;L\,d(t,s)\qquad\forall\,s,t\in T.‖Xt−Xs‖p≤L​d​(t,s)∀s,t∈T.\|X_{t}-X_{s}\|_{p}\;\leq\;L\,d(t,s)\qquad\forall\,s,t\in T.

(E.83)



Then






\mathbb{E}\!\left[\sup_{s,t\in T}\bigl(X_{t}-X_{s}\bigr)\right]\;\leq\;C\,L\int_{0}^{\Delta}\bigl({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}(T,d,\varepsilon)\bigr)^{1/p}\,d\varepsilon,𝔼⁡[sups,t∈T(Xt−Xs)]≤C​L​∫0Δ(𝒩⁡(T,d,ε))1/p​𝑑ε,\mathbb{E}\!\left[\sup_{s,t\in T}\bigl(X_{t}-X_{s}\bigr)\right]\;\leq\;C\,L\int_{0}^{\Delta}\bigl({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}(T,d,\varepsilon)\bigr)^{1/p}\,d\varepsilon,

(E.84)



where {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}(T,d,\varepsilon)𝒩⁡(T,d,ε){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}(T,d,\varepsilon) is the \varepsilonε\varepsilon-covering number and C<\inftyC<∞C<\infty is an absolute constant.








E.3 Concentration bounds for  icl (icl)




We now apply the moment symmetrization results to derive concentration bounds for icl in the dependent data setting.
These concentration bounds will then be translated into generalization bounds in the next subsection.

(E.88)






In the second assumption, the Wasserstein distance between the conditional distributions of {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}xt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}} given {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}xs{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}} and {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}^{\prime}xs′{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}^{\prime} is assumed to be controlled by the norm of the task {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}θ{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}.
This is a slight difference with Assumption 2 where we assumed a dependence on 1+\lVert{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\rVert1+∥θ∥1+\lVert{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\rVert. This is however without loss of generality as we can always consider \widetilde{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}}=(1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}+1}θ~=(1,θ)∈ℝd+1\widetilde{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}}=(1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}+1} and redefine the task distribution accordingly and this cosmetic change simplifies the presentation.
We could also consider a dependence on \lVert{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}^{\prime}\rVert∥xs−xs′∥\lVert{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}^{\prime}\rVert, see Theorem E.1, but we omit this for simplicity.



Assumption 6 (Finite moments of the task distribution).


There exists {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}q}\geq 2q≥2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}q}\geq 2 integer such that \ex[\lVert{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\rVert^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}q}}]<+\infty𝔼⁡[∥θ∥q]<+∞\ex[\lVert{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\theta}\rVert^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}q}}]<+\infty.





Our theory could be extended to more general assumptions on the distributions of sample, but, for simplicity, we will make the following sub-Gaussian assumption on the data, conditionally on the past data and the task. Hence, this assumption does not restrict the task distribution in any way.

E.4 Complexity bounds for ICL




We now derive bounds for the analogue of the Rademacher complexity term in our setting.
We will again rely on Theorem E.1.

Appendix F Additional details on examples





F.1 Example: Volterra equation model




We discuss the Volterra equation model to explicit the dependence of the generalization bounds on the memory decay parameter \alpha>0α>0\alpha>0.




Setup.



Let (W_{t})_{t\geq 1}(Wt)t≥1(W_{t})_{t\geq 1} be noise sequence taking values in \mathbb{R}^{d}ℝd\mathbb{R}^{d}.
Given a Lipschitz drift b\,,mathbbR^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}b,m​a​t​h​b​b​Rd→ℝdb\,,mathbbR^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d} with Lipschitz constant L\geq 0L≥0L\geq 0,
we consider the discretized Volterra equation: for t\geq 0t≥0t\geq 0,






X_{t+1}\;=\;\sum_{u=1}^{t}K(t,u)\,\bigl(b(X_{u})+W_{u}\bigr),\qquad K(t,u)\;=\;\frac{1}{(t-u+1)^{\alpha}},\ \ \alpha>0.Xt+1=∑u=1tK⁡(t,u)​(b⁡(Xu)+Wu),K⁡(t,u)=1(t−u+1)α,α>0.X_{t+1}\;=\;\sum_{u=1}^{t}K(t,u)\,\bigl(b(X_{u})+W_{u}\bigr),\qquad K(t,u)\;=\;\frac{1}{(t-u+1)^{\alpha}},\ \ \alpha>0.

(F.1)





When applying the generalization framework, we would consider the augmented sequence (X_{1},W_{1},X_{2},W_{2},\ldots)(X1,W1,X2,W2,…)(X_{1},W_{1},X_{2},W_{2},\ldots).
To satisfy the weak dependence assumption Assumption 5, we need to bound the effect of perturbations in either the state or the noise or the drift.
We begin with perturbations in the state or noise, and we discuss drift perturbations at the end of this section.
For perturbations in the state or noise, we will obtain bounds on the Wasserstein distance between the conditional laws of X_{t}XtX_{t} and X^{\prime}_{t}Xt′X^{\prime}_{t} given the past, where X_{t}XtX_{t} and X^{\prime}_{t}Xt′X^{\prime}_{t} are two versions of the process (F.1) that differ by a perturbation at some time s<ts<ts<t.




The coefficient \alphaα\alpha will play a key role in the dependence structure through the sums:






H_{\alpha}(n)\;=\;\sum_{r=1}^{n}\frac{1}{r^{\alpha}}.Hα​(n)=∑r=1n1rα.H_{\alpha}(n)\;=\;\sum_{r=1}^{n}\frac{1}{r^{\alpha}}.

(F.2)



We also use \zeta(\alpha)=\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}r^{-\alpha}ζ⁡(α)=∑r=1∞r−α\zeta(\alpha)=\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}r^{-\alpha} for \alpha>1α>1\alpha>1 and we have the following bounds on H_{\alpha}(n)Hα​(n)H_{\alpha}(n)






H_{\alpha}(n)\;\leq\;\begin{cases}1+\log n,&\alpha=1,\\[4.0pt]
\zeta(\alpha),&\alpha>1.\end{cases}Hα​(n)≤{1+log⁡n,α=1,ζ⁡(α),α>1.H_{\alpha}(n)\;\leq\;\begin{cases}1+\log n,&\alpha=1,\\[4.0pt]
\zeta(\alpha),&\alpha>1.\end{cases}

(F.3)





We will make use of the following technical lemma.



Lemma F.1.


Let (a_{n})_{n\geq 0}(an)n≥0(a_{n})_{n\geq 0} be nonnegative numbers and suppose that for n\geq 1n≥1n\geq 1,






a_{n}\;\leq\;L\sum_{r=1}^{n}r^{-\alpha}\,a_{n-r}\;+\;g_{n},an≤L​∑r=1nr−α​an−r+gn,a_{n}\;\leq\;L\sum_{r=1}^{n}r^{-\alpha}\,a_{n-r}\;+\;g_{n},

(F.4)



with non-decreasing (g_{n})_{n\geq 1}(gn)n≥1(g_{n})_{n\geq 1} and given a_{0}\geq 0a0≥0a_{0}\geq 0.
Define, for N\geq 1N≥1N\geq 1,






\lambda_{N}\coloneqq\begin{cases}L(1+\log N)&\text{ if }\alpha=1,\\[6.0pt]
L\zeta(\alpha)&\text{ if }\alpha>1.\end{cases}λN≔{L⁡(1+log⁡N) if ​α=1,L​ζ​(α) if ​α>1.\lambda_{N}\coloneqq\begin{cases}L(1+\log N)&\text{ if }\alpha=1,\\[6.0pt]
L\zeta(\alpha)&\text{ if }\alpha>1.\end{cases}

(F.5)



Then, for all 1\leq n\leq N1≤n≤N1\leq n\leq N,






a_{n}\;\leq\;\lambda_{N}^{n}a_{0}\;+\;\sum_{j=1}^{n}g_{j}{\lambda_{N}^{n-j}}.an≤λNn​a0+∑j=1ngj​λNn−j.a_{n}\;\leq\;\lambda_{N}^{n}a_{0}\;+\;\sum_{j=1}^{n}g_{j}{\lambda_{N}^{n-j}}.

(F.6)





Proof.


Let A_{n}:=\max_{0\leq m\leq n}a_{m}An:=max0≤m≤n⁡amA_{n}:=\max_{0\leq m\leq n}a_{m}. From (F.4),
a_{n}\leq L\sum_{r=1}^{n}r^{-\alpha}A_{n-r}+g_{n}\leq LH_{\alpha}(n)A_{n-1}+g_{n}an≤L​∑r=1nr−α​An−r+gn≤L​Hα​(n)​An−1+gna_{n}\leq L\sum_{r=1}^{n}r^{-\alpha}A_{n-r}+g_{n}\leq LH_{\alpha}(n)A_{n-1}+g_{n}, so A_{n}\leq LH_{\alpha}(n)A_{n-1}+g_{n}An≤L​Hα​(n)​An−1+gnA_{n}\leq LH_{\alpha}(n)A_{n-1}+g_{n} since (g_{n})_{n}(gn)n(g_{n})_{n} is non-decreasing.
Bounding H_{\alpha}(n)Hα​(n)H_{\alpha}(n) using (F.3) gives A_{n}\leq\lambda_{N}A_{n-1}+g_{n}An≤λN​An−1+gnA_{n}\leq\lambda_{N}A_{n-1}+g_{n} for all 1\leq n\leq N1≤n≤N1\leq n\leq N.
Iterating this inequality yields the result.
∎







State perturbation.



Fix s\geq 1s≥1s\geq 1 and let \mathcal{F}_{s}:=\sigma\big(X_{1},\dots,X_{s},\ W_{1},\dots,W_{s}\big)ℱs:=σ⁡(X1,…,Xs,W1,…,Ws)\mathcal{F}_{s}:=\sigma\big(X_{1},\dots,X_{s},\ W_{1},\dots,W_{s}\big) on which we condition.
Assume the two systems agree up to s-1s−1s-1, and at time sss we have






X^{\prime}_{s}\;=\;X_{s}-hXs′=Xs−hX^{\prime}_{s}\;=\;X_{s}-h




with h\neq 0h≠0h\neq 0.
For t\geq st≥st\geq s, define \Delta_{t}:=X_{t}-X^{\prime}_{t}Δt:=Xt−Xt′\Delta_{t}:=X_{t}-X^{\prime}_{t}.
Subtracting (F.1) for the two evolutions (they share (W_{u})(Wu)(W_{u})) gives for t\geq st≥st\geq s:






\Delta_{t+1}=\sum_{u=s}^{t}\frac{b(X_{u})-b(X^{\prime}_{u})}{(t-u+1)^{\alpha}},\qquad\|\Delta_{t+1}\|\leq L\sum_{u=s}^{t}\frac{\|\Delta_{u}\|}{(t-u+1)^{\alpha}}.Δt+1=∑u=stb⁡(Xu)−b⁡(Xu′)(t−u+1)α,‖Δt+1‖≤L​∑u=st‖Δu‖(t−u+1)α.\Delta_{t+1}=\sum_{u=s}^{t}\frac{b(X_{u})-b(X^{\prime}_{u})}{(t-u+1)^{\alpha}},\qquad\|\Delta_{t+1}\|\leq L\sum_{u=s}^{t}\frac{\|\Delta_{u}\|}{(t-u+1)^{\alpha}}.

(F.7)



Set n:=t-s+1n:=t−s+1n:=t-s+1, a_{n}:=\mathbb{E}\big(\|\Delta_{s+n}\|\,\big|\,\mathcal{F}_{s}\big)an:=𝔼⁡(‖Δs+n‖|ℱs)a_{n}:=\mathbb{E}\big(\|\Delta_{s+n}\|\,\big|\,\mathcal{F}_{s}\big) and a_{0}=\|\Delta_{s}\|=\|h\|a0=‖Δs‖=‖h‖a_{0}=\|\Delta_{s}\|=\|h\|.
Applying Lemma F.1 with g_{n}=0gn=0g_{n}=0 yields, for n\leq Nn≤Nn\leq N,






a_{n}\;\leq\;\lambda_{N}^{n}\,\|h\|,an≤λNn​‖h‖,a_{n}\;\leq\;\lambda_{N}^{n}\,\|h\|,

(F.8)



We now bound the Wasserstein distance between the conditional laws of X_{s+n}Xs+nX_{s+n} and X^{\prime}_{s+n}Xs+n′X^{\prime}_{s+n} given \mathcal{F}_{s}ℱs\mathcal{F}_{s} by using the synchronous coupling between X_{s+n}Xs+nX_{s+n} and X^{\prime}_{s+n}Xs+n′X^{\prime}_{s+n} (which share the same noise sequence (W_{u})_{u>s}(Wu)u>s(W_{u})_{u>s}):






W_{1}\!\big(\mathcal{L}(X_{s+n}\mid\mathcal{F}_{s}),\ \mathcal{L}(X^{\prime}_{s+n}\mid\mathcal{F}_{s})\big)\leq\mathbb{E}\big(\|X_{s+n}-X^{\prime}_{s+n}\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{s}\big)\leq\lambda_{N}^{n}\,\|h\|.W1​(ℒ⁡(Xs+n∣ℱs),ℒ⁡(Xs+n′∣ℱs))≤𝔼⁡(‖Xs+n−Xs+n′‖∣ℱs)≤λNn​‖h‖.W_{1}\!\big(\mathcal{L}(X_{s+n}\mid\mathcal{F}_{s}),\ \mathcal{L}(X^{\prime}_{s+n}\mid\mathcal{F}_{s})\big)\leq\mathbb{E}\big(\|X_{s+n}-X^{\prime}_{s+n}\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{s}\big)\leq\lambda_{N}^{n}\,\|h\|.




Therefore, for any horizon T\geq s+1T≥s+1T\geq s+1,






{\quad\sup_{\,s+1\leq t\leq T}W_{1}\!\big(\mathcal{L}(X_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{s}),\ \mathcal{L}(X^{\prime}_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{s})\big)\;\leq\;\|h\|\,\lambda_{T-s}^{T-s}=\begin{cases}\|h\|\,(L(1+\log(T-s))^{T-s}&\text{if }\alpha=1,\\[6.0pt]
\|h\|\,(L\zeta(\alpha))^{T-s}&\text{if }\alpha>1.\end{cases}\quad}sups+1≤t≤TW1​(ℒ⁡(Xt∣ℱs),ℒ⁡(Xt′∣ℱs))≤|h|λT−sT−s={‖h‖​(L​(1+log⁡(T−s))T−sCLOSEif ​α=1,‖h‖​(L​ζ​(α))T−sif ​α>1.{\quad\sup_{\,s+1\leq t\leq T}W_{1}\!\big(\mathcal{L}(X_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{s}),\ \mathcal{L}(X^{\prime}_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{s})\big)\;\leq\;\|h\|\,\lambda_{T-s}^{T-s}=\begin{cases}\|h\|\,(L(1+\log(T-s))^{T-s}&\text{if }\alpha=1,\\[6.0pt]
\|h\|\,(L\zeta(\alpha))^{T-s}&\text{if }\alpha>1.\end{cases}\quad}

(F.9)





The behaviour of the bound crucially depends on \alphaα\alpha and LLL: if \alpha>1α>1\alpha>1 and L\zeta(\alpha)<1L​ζ​(α)<1L\zeta(\alpha)<1, the effect of the perturbation decays exponentially fast with T-sT−sT-s; if \alpha>1α>1\alpha>1 and L\zeta(\alpha)>1L​ζ​(α)>1L\zeta(\alpha)>1, the effect of the perturbation grows exponentially fast with T-sT−sT-s.
In both case, higher values of \alphaα\alpha (faster memory decay) lead to better dependence properties.






Noise perturbation.



Fix s\geq 1s≥1s\geq 1 and let \mathcal{F}_{s-1}:=\sigma\big(X_{1},\dots,X_{s-1},\ W_{1},\dots,W_{s-1}\big)ℱs−1:=σ⁡(X1,…,Xs−1,W1,…,Ws−1)\mathcal{F}_{s-1}:=\sigma\big(X_{1},\dots,X_{s-1},\ W_{1},\dots,W_{s-1}\big).

W^{\prime}_{s}\;=\;W_{s}+\etaWs′=Ws+ηW^{\prime}_{s}\;=\;W_{s}+\eta




with \eta\neq 0η≠0\eta\neq 0,
and W^{\prime}_{u}=W_{u}Wu′=WuW^{\prime}_{u}=W_{u} for u\neq su≠su\neq s.

\Delta_{t+1}=\sum_{u=s}^{t}\frac{b(X_{u})-b(X^{\prime}_{u})}{(t-u+1)^{\alpha}}\;+\;\frac{W_{s}-W^{\prime}_{s}}{(t-s+1)^{\alpha}}.Δt+1=∑u=stb⁡(Xu)−b⁡(Xu′)(t−u+1)α+Ws−Ws′(t−s+1)α.\Delta_{t+1}=\sum_{u=s}^{t}\frac{b(X_{u})-b(X^{\prime}_{u})}{(t-u+1)^{\alpha}}\;+\;\frac{W_{s}-W^{\prime}_{s}}{(t-s+1)^{\alpha}}.

(F.10)



Taking norms and using Lipschitzness,






\|\Delta_{t+1}\|\;\leq\;L\sum_{u=s}^{t}\frac{\|\Delta_{u}\|}{(t-u+1)^{\alpha}}\;+\;\frac{\|\eta\|}{(t-s+1)^{\alpha}}.‖Δt+1‖≤L​∑u=st‖Δu‖(t−u+1)α+‖η‖(t−s+1)α.\|\Delta_{t+1}\|\;\leq\;L\sum_{u=s}^{t}\frac{\|\Delta_{u}\|}{(t-u+1)^{\alpha}}\;+\;\frac{\|\eta\|}{(t-s+1)^{\alpha}}.




Set n:=t-s+1n:=t−s+1n:=t-s+1 and a_{n}:=\mathbb{E}\big(\|\Delta_{s+n}\|\,\big|\,\mathcal{F}_{s-1}\big)an:=𝔼⁡(‖Δs+n‖|ℱs−1)a_{n}:=\mathbb{E}\big(\|\Delta_{s+n}\|\,\big|\,\mathcal{F}_{s-1}\big).

a_{n}\;\leq\;\sum_{j=1}^{n}\|\eta\|j^{-\alpha}\,{\lambda_{N}^{n-j}}\leq\|\eta\|\times\frac{\lambda_{N}^{n}-1}{\lambda_{N}-1}\,,an≤∑j=1n‖η‖​j−α​λNn−j≤‖η‖×λNn−1λN−1,a_{n}\;\leq\;\sum_{j=1}^{n}\|\eta\|j^{-\alpha}\,{\lambda_{N}^{n-j}}\leq\|\eta\|\times\frac{\lambda_{N}^{n}-1}{\lambda_{N}-1}\,,

(F.11)



where we consider \lambda_{N}\neq 1λN≠1\lambda_{N}\neq 1 for simplicity.




Bounding the Wasserstein distance as before yields, for any horizon T\geq s+1T≥s+1T\geq s+1,






{\quad\sup_{\,s+1\leq t\leq T}W_{1}\!\big(\mathcal{L}(X_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{s-1}),\ \mathcal{L}(X^{\prime}_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{s-1})\big)\;\leq\;\begin{cases}\|\eta\|\,\frac{(L(1+\log(T-s)))^{T-s}-1}{L(1+\log(T-s))-1},&\text{if }\alpha=1,\\[6.0pt]

Drift perturbation.



To consider drift perturbations, we write the drift as b_{\theta}bθb_{\theta} where \thetaθ\theta is a parameter. F.2 Examples for task selection assumptions




In this section, we check that the examples of Section 3.1 in the main text satisfy Assumptions 3 and 4.
These are lengthy but mostly straightforward calculations, which we sketch to illustrate how to verify the assumptions in practice.



Example F.1 (Linear regression).


We consider the linear regression example of Section 3.1 in the main text and check that it satisfies Assumptions 3 and 4.

Define Q\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}\times d}Q∈ℝT×dQ\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{R}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}\times d} has rows {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}q}_{t}^{\top}qt⊤{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}q}_{t}^{\top} and Y=({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y}_{t})_{t=1}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T}Y=(yt)t=1TY=({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y}_{t})_{t=1}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}T},

where the constant term depends on QQQ but not on \thetaθ\theta




Let us begin with the tail behavior.

Example F.2 (Ornstein–Uhlenbeck process).

We are continuing to improve HTML versions of papers, and your feedback helps enhance accessibility and mobile
      support. To report errors in the HTML that will help us improve conversion and rendering, choose any of the
      methods listed below:

Tip: You can select the relevant text first, to include it in your report.

    
Our team has already identified the following issues. We appreciate your time reviewing and reporting rendering errors we
      may not have found yet. Your efforts will help us improve the HTML versions for all readers, because disability
      should not be a barrier to accessing research. Thank you for your continued support in championing open access for
      all.
